Instability under contentless doubt tracks intrinsic item ambiguity rather than evidence availability, and so does not serve as a grounding signal. Flips are almost entirely corrupting (99/100 across both levels). The model is not unaware of the missing evidence---it flags the blank image at baseline on 35 of 60 items and abstains on 4--6---yet those abstentions do not survive contact with pressure. None of the epistemic signals a user could plausibly consult distinguishes a grounded diagnosis from a confabulated one.

摘要：

##### **StaX-Net: Stackelberg Interleaved Asymmetric Network for Cross-Modality Medical Image Translation**
2610.34214v1 by Amin Golnari, Saeid Sanei

Cross-modality medical image translation is challenged by anatomically inconsistent synthesis that can compromise clinical diagnosis. We propose StaX-Net, a Stackelberg interleaved asymmetric network that incorporates a partially coupled encoder-discriminator structure, and a 2D feature aggregation and spatial transformation (FAST) layer that distills target-supervised priors under the learning using privileged information paradigm. The shared blocks between the generator and discriminator enhance architectural feature alignment. To stabilize the leader-follower training, a Stackelberg-inspired asymmetric optimization scheme employs explicit shared-parameter freezing and asymmetric learning rates. The FAST layer distills target-domain priors during training and applies a source-only transform at inference. This constrains the synthesis process through target-informed feature representations relative to standard generative architectures. The code is available on GitHub for public use.

摘要：

##### **Fed-Universe: A Semantic-Geometric-Topological-Human (S-G-T-H) Stack for Negotiated Alignment in Federated Systems**
2610.34009v1 by Ting Xu, Henry Leung

Conventional federated learners implicitly optimize for global means, creating a "one-size-fits-all" paradigm that inevitably suppresses the minority under cross-site heterogeneity. To bridge this critical gap, we present Fed-Universe, a generalizable Semantic-Geometric-Topological-Human (S-G-T-H) architecture that transforms passive averaging into an active negotiation for decentralized alignment. The S-layer deploys an edge-capable LLM as a semantic surrogate to translate heterogeneous inputs into standardized patient profile prompts. The G-layer enforces geometric quality assurance (GQA) via a mapping f: Theta -> Q from the statistical parameter space to a physically interpretable quality domain, employing a cosine similarity gate to retain minority updates based on physical geometry rather than statistical proximity. The T-layer executes topological Pareto control, identifying a sensitivity-based knee point (lambda_k) to balance multi-objective aggregation without majority degradation. Finally, the H-layer operationalizes a cognitive twin dynamic to mirror the user's real-time psychological state, dynamically adapting human-computer interaction modes via active intent alignment. To ground this theoretical architecture empirically, we validated the S-G-T core on a binational synthetic clinical network simulation. This proof-of-concept demonstrated a "zero-sum escape": a minority node (<3% data volume) achieved utility parity (loss reduction from 0.857 to 0.340) alongside dominant hubs. Our Fed-Universe framework is being expanded along two focused pillars: institutional multimodal alignment (Fed-Ultra) and individual cognitive intent alignment (Fed-Human).

摘要：

##### **Are We Done with UMLS Injection into Language Models for Medical Question Answering?**
2610.33801v1 by Yiran Zeng, Giacomo Frisoni, Lorenzo Molfetta, Gianluca Moro

Maybe not. The Unified Medical Language System (UMLS) has become an oracle for millions of clinicians and health system developers worldwide. Researchers have increasingly used knowledge graphs derived from UMLS to enhance language models' factual accuracy and reasoning traceability in high-stakes tasks. Medical Question Answering (MQA) is a prime testbed for integration, with benchmarks usually involving professional exercises such as disease diagnosis and drug prescription from patient history. Despite the assumed benefits, empirical evidence shows that UMLS-based graph injection typically yields modest performance gains. This paper reviews existing MQA work, critically analyzing the selected UMLS sources and their usage with language models, and probes each of these choices in isolation. We uncover methodological issues never discussed in prior work, from low UMLS coverage to weak subgraph retrieval pipelines. Based on our findings, we provide actionable recommendations to unlock the untapped potential of UMLS as an external knowledge source.
